</div>

<div id="A1" class="section ltx_appendix">

### <span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_appendix">Appendix A </span>Limitations & Future Works

<div id="A1.p1" class="ltx_para">

Our work addresses the context management problem in long-horizon LLM agents and proposes a
framework for optimizing context compression. While the method effectively reduces token costs with
minimal performance degradation, it also presents several limitations and potentials for future
works.

</div>

<div id="A1.SS0.SSS0.Px1" class="section ltx_paragraph">

##### Scope of empirical evaluation.

<div id="A1.SS0.SSS0.Px1.p1" class="ltx_para">

Our work primarily focuses on the GPT models due to computational and budgetary constraints. While
the proposed framework is designed to be model-agnostic, its empirical generalizability to other
foundation models, such as Gemini (<a href="#bib.bib54" class="ltx_ref">5</a>) or
Claude (<a href="#bib.bib53" class="ltx_ref">2</a>), has yet to be extensively verified.
Furthermore, the inclusion of massive open-source models like
DeepSeek-R1 (<a href="#bib.bib11" class="ltx_ref">6</a>) or Qwen3-235B
(<a href="#bib.bib48" class="ltx_ref">51</a>) was restricted by GPU availability. Extending the
analysis to these models would provide stronger evidence of robustness and broaden the applicability
of our conclusions.

</div>

</div>

<div id="A1.SS0.SSS0.Px2" class="section ltx_paragraph">

##### Real-world deployability

<div id="A1.SS0.SSS0.Px2.p1" class="ltx_para">

Although our validation on three distinct benchmarks reflects realistic agentic scenarios, these
environments remain controlled. Transitioning from benchmark-centric evaluations to in-the-wild
deployment—where task complexity is stochastic and environmental constraints are less
predictable—remains an open challenge. We identify the integration of our framework into live,
multi-agent production systems as a valuable next step.

</div>

</div>

<div id="A1.SS0.SSS0.Px3" class="section ltx_paragraph">

##### Convergence of natural language space optimization

<div id="A1.SS0.SSS0.Px3.p1" class="ltx_para">

The optimization of our compression guidelines relies on LLM-generated feedback, a strategy aligned
with recent prompt optimization work (<a href="#bib.bib40" class="ltx_ref">58</a>;
<a href="#bib.bib39" class="ltx_ref">15</a>; <a href="#bib.bib43" class="ltx_ref">29</a>;
<a href="#bib.bib38" class="ltx_ref">52</a>). However, unlike traditional numerical gradient-based
optimization, this approach lacks a formal convergence guarantee. While our method of sampling
multiple candidates and selecting the best performer partially mitigates this, it does not provide a
principled theoretical foundation. A deeper analysis of the natural language space optimization
process and more principled methods for optimizing the objective
in <a href="#S3.SS3" class="ltx_ref"
title="3.3 Optimizing Compression Guidelines ‣ 3 Agent Context Optimization (Acon) ‣ Acon: Optimizing Context Compression for Long-horizon LLM Agents"><span
class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">3.3</span></a> would be valuable directions for future work.

</div>

</div>

<div id="A1.SS0.SSS0.Px4" class="section ltx_paragraph">

##### Distillation gap and data scalability

<div id="A1.SS0.SSS0.Px4.p1" class="ltx_para">

A performance gap remains between our distilled models and their teacher models. We hypothesize that
our current dataset (100 examples per domain) may not fully capture the reasoning required for
complete behavioral cloning. Future work will investigate whether increasing the diversity and
volume of training data can eliminate the residual performance gap.

</div>

</div>

<div id="A1.SS0.SSS0.Px5" class="section ltx_paragraph">

##### Latency and KV-cache dynamics

<div id="A1.SS0.SSS0.Px5.p1" class="ltx_para">

A significant, yet often overlooked, challenge is the computational overhead inherent in the
compression process itself—a limitation shared by the majority of existing context compression
frameworks (<a href="#bib.bib15" class="ltx_ref">20</a>;
<a href="#bib.bib19" class="ltx_ref">35</a>; <a href="#bib.bib17" class="ltx_ref">53</a>;
<a href="#bib.bib58" class="ltx_ref">36</a>; <a href="#bib.bib28" class="ltx_ref">61</a>;
<a href="#bib.bib56" class="ltx_ref">55</a>). In transformer-based architectures, history
compression typically invalidates the existing KV-cache, necessitating a costly re-computation of
the entire compressed sequence. While observation compression can mitigate this by reducing the
context before caching them, the generative overhead required for the compression procedure itself
still introduces latency, often slowing down the agent’s response time
(<a href="#bib.bib15" class="ltx_ref">20</a>). Consequently, the field currently faces a trade-off
where the gains in long-horizon efficiency are partially offset by the increased computational cost
of the compression itself. To move beyond these constraints, the development of KV-cache eviction or
compression strategies—specifically optimized for the dynamic, multi-turn nature of agentic
workflows—can be a potential avenue for future research, building upon foundational works in
long-context modeling (<a href="#bib.bib45" class="ltx_ref">59</a>;
<a href="#bib.bib52" class="ltx_ref">46</a>; <a href="#bib.bib51" class="ltx_ref">16</a>).

</div>

</div>

</div>

<div id="A2" class="section ltx_appendix">

### <span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_appendix">Appendix B </span>Experimental Setup Details

<div id="A2.SS1" class="section ltx_subsection">

#### <span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_subsection">B.1 </span>Datasets

<div id="A2.SS1.SSS0.Px1" class="section ltx_paragraph">

##### AppWorld (<a href="#bib.bib34" class="ltx_ref">39</a>).

<div id="A2.SS1.SSS0.Px1.p1" class="ltx_para">

AppWorld is our primary benchmark, providing a high-quality execution environment that integrates
nine everyday applications (Spotify, SimpleNote, Amazon, Venmo, Gmail, Splitwise, File system,
Todoist, and Phone) through 457 APIs. It also includes realistic simulations of approximately 100
functional users. This benchmark is particularly suitable for evaluating long-horizon productivity
agents, as its multi-step tasks require an average of 42.5 API calls per task. We follow the
official split, using 90 training tasks for guideline optimization and distillation, and 168
test-normal tasks for evaluation. An example trajectory from AppWorld is provided in
Example <a href="#A4.SS0.SSS0.Px3" class="ltx_ref"
title="Compressed observations. ‣ Appendix D Qualitative Examples ‣ Acon: Optimizing Context Compression for Long-horizon LLM Agents"><span
class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">D</span></a>.

</div>

</div>

<div id="A2.SS1.SSS0.Px2" class="section ltx_paragraph">

##### OfficeBench (<a href="#bib.bib35" class="ltx_ref">44</a>).

<div id="A2.SS1.SSS0.Px2.p1" class="ltx_para">

OfficeBench is a benchmark for office automation using applications such as Word, Excel, PDF,
Calendar, Email, Shell, and Calculator. It evaluates the ability of agents to coordinate across
multiple apps to complete complex tasks, making it well suited for long-horizon scenarios. Tasks are
categorized as 1-app, 2-app, or 3-app depending on the number of applications required. We restrict
our experiments to text-related tasks, excluding those requiring OCR, as OCR quality could confound
the evaluation. Since no official split is available, we randomly partition the tasks into training
and test sets with a 1:1 ratio, resulting in 92 training tasks and 95 test tasks. We additionally
refine the dataset by removing ambiguous tasks and ensuring that synthetic files (testbeds) are not
shared across splits.

</div>

</div>

<div id="A2.SS1.SSS0.Px3" class="section ltx_paragraph">

##### 8-Objective QA (<a href="#bib.bib28" class="ltx_ref">61</a>).

<div id="A2.SS1.SSS0.Px3.p1" class="ltx_para">

The 8-objective QA benchmark simulates deep research-style agentic tasks. Unlike conventional
multi-hop QA, which requires answering a single question using multiple pieces of evidence, this
benchmark poses eight distinct questions within one task, and the agent must provide answers to all
of them at the end. This design creates a more challenging setting for long-horizon agents.
Following <a href="#bib.bib28" class="ltx_ref">61</a>, we construct each task by grouping eight
questions together. Questions are drawn from
NaturalQuestions (<a href="#bib.bib44" class="ltx_ref">19</a>), resulting in 100 training tasks
(from the train split) and 100 test tasks (from the test split). For retrieval, we use a BM25
retriever over the 2018 Wikipedia knowledge base,
following <a href="#bib.bib50" class="ltx_ref">14</a>.

</div>

<div id="A2.SS1.SSS0.Px3.p2" class="ltx_para">

We include the example task of each benchmark
in <a href="#A2.T5" class="ltx_ref ltx_refmacro_autoref" title="Table 5 ‣ 8-Objective QA (
,
). ‣ B.1 Datasets ‣ Appendix B Experimental Setup Details ‣ Acon: Optimizing Context Compression for Long-horizon LLM Agents"><span
class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">Table 5</span></a>.

</div>

<td id="A2.T5.5.9.2" class="ltx_td ltx_align_left"
style="padding-top: 1.5pt; padding-bottom: 1.5pt"><span id="A2.T5.5.9.2.1"
class="ltx_inline-block ltx_align_top"> <span id="A2.T5.5.9.2.1.1" class="ltx_p">Read company
revenues and send an email with subject <span id="A2.T5.5.9.2.1.1.1"
class="ltx_text ltx_font_typewriter">revenues</span>, containing data to Bob for reporting, also
write a <span id="A2.T5.5.9.2.1.1.2" class="ltx_text ltx_font_typewriter">revenue.docx</span> to
summarize it.</span> </span></td>
</tr>
<tr id="A2.T5.5.10" class="ltx_tr">
<td colspan="2" id="A2.T5.5.10.1" class="ltx_td ltx_align_left ltx_th ltx_th_row ltx_border_t"
style="padding-top: 1.5pt; padding-bottom: 1.5pt"><span id="A2.T5.5.10.1.1"
class="ltx_text ltx_font_bold">8-objective QA</span></td>
</tr>
<tr id="A2.T5.5.11" class="ltx_tr">
<td id="A2.T5.5.11.1" class="ltx_td ltx_align_left ltx_th ltx_th_row ltx_border_bb"
style="padding-top: 1.5pt; padding-bottom: 1.5pt">–</td>
<td id="A2.T5.5.11.2" class="ltx_td ltx_align_left ltx_border_bb"
style="padding-top: 1.5pt; padding-bottom: 1.5pt"><span id="A2.T5.5.11.2.1"
class="ltx_inline-block ltx_align_top"> <span id="A2.T5.5.11.2.1.1" class="ltx_p">who wrote the song
<em>Oceans Where Feet May Fail</em>?; who plays Eddie the Eagle in the movie?; when was the last
time England were in the final of World Cup?; who plays Chelsea’s mom on <em>Young and the
Restless</em>?; what is the largest coin in the US?; who sang <em>Even the Bad Times Are Good</em>?;
who sings <em>This Is My Town</em> country song?; which of the Guianas is not an independent

</tr>
</tbody>
</table>
<figcaption><span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_table"><span id="A2.T5.3" class="ltx_text"
style="font-size:90%;">Table 5</span>: </span><span id="A2.T5.4" class="ltx_text"
style="font-size:90%;">Example tasks across benchmarks.</span></figcaption>
</figure>

</div>

</div>

<div id="A2.SS2" class="section ltx_subsection">

#### <span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_subsection">B.2 </span>Evaluation Metrics

<div id="A2.SS2.p1" class="ltx_para">

For efficiency evaluation, we adopt two metrics—*peak tokens* and *dependency*—introduced in
LightThinker (<a href="#bib.bib45" class="ltx_ref">59</a>) and
MEM1 (<a href="#bib.bib28" class="ltx_ref">61</a>).

</div>

<div id="A2.SS2.SSS0.Px1" class="section ltx_paragraph">

##### Peak tokens.

<div id="A2.SS2.SSS0.Px1.p1" class="ltx_para">

Peak tokens are measured as the maximum number of tokens observed in any single sequence throughout
the agent’s trajectory, excluding system prompts. This metric serves as a proxy for inference-time
memory requirements and corresponds to the maximum peak shown
in <a href="#S1.F2" class="ltx_ref ltx_refmacro_autoref"
title="Figure 2 ‣ 1 Introduction ‣ Acon: Optimizing Context Compression for Long-horizon LLM Agents"><span
class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">Figure 2</span></a>.

</div>

</div>

<div id="A2.SS2.SSS0.Px2" class="section ltx_paragraph">

##### Dependency.

<div id="A2.SS2.SSS0.Px2.p1" class="ltx_para">

Dependency is defined as the area under the curve
in <a href="#S1.F2" class="ltx_ref ltx_refmacro_autoref"
title="Figure 2 ‣ 1 Introduction ‣ Acon: Optimizing Context Compression for Long-horizon LLM Agents"><span
class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">Figure 2</span></a>. At each step $`t`$, given the number of input
tokens $`n_{i}^{(t)}`$ and output tokens $`n_{o}^{(t)}`$, it is calculated as:

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\sum_{t\in[T]}\frac{(n_{i}^{(t)}+2n_{o}^{(t)})\times n_{o}^{(t)}}{2}.
``` |  | <span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_equation ltx_align_right">(12)</span> |

This metric approximates the cumulative computational cost incurred by action generation across the
trajectory.

</div>

</div>

<div id="A2.SS2.SSS0.Px3" class="section ltx_paragraph">

##### API Cost.

<div id="A2.SS2.SSS0.Px3.p1" class="ltx_para">

For the cost analysis, we use the official OpenAI pricing (as of September 2025) for
<span id="A2.SS2.SSS0.Px3.p1.1.1" class="ltx_text ltx_font_typewriter">gpt-4.1</span> and
<span id="A2.SS2.SSS0.Px3.p1.1.2"
class="ltx_text ltx_font_typewriter">gpt-4.1-mini</span> (<a href="#bib.bib7" class="ltx_ref">24</a>).
Specifically, <span id="A2.SS2.SSS0.Px3.p1.1.3" class="ltx_text ltx_font_typewriter">gpt-4.1</span>
is priced at \$3.00 per 1M input tokens and \$12.00 per 1M output tokens. For
<span id="A2.SS2.SSS0.Px3.p1.1.4" class="ltx_text ltx_font_typewriter">gpt-4.1-mini</span>, the
costs are \$0.80 per 1M input tokens and \$3.20 per 1M output tokens. For
<span id="A2.SS2.SSS0.Px3.p1.1.5"
class="ltx_text ltx_font_typewriter">Qwen3-14B</span> (<a href="#bib.bib48" class="ltx_ref">51</a>),
since no official API pricing is available, we approximate the cost using
OpenRouter<span id="footnote1"
class="ltx_note ltx_role_footnote"><sup>1</sup><span class="ltx_note_outer"><span class="ltx_note_content"><sup>1</sup>
<span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_note">1</span> <a href="https://openrouter.ai/"
class="ltx_ref ltx_url ltx_font_typewriter">https://openrouter.ai/</a></span></span></span>: \$0.06
per 1M input tokens and \$0.24 per 1M output tokens.

</div>

</div>

</div>

<div id="A2.SS3" class="section ltx_subsection">

#### <span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_subsection">B.3 </span>Implementation Details & Hyperparameters

<div id="A2.SS3.SSS0.Px1" class="section ltx_paragraph">

##### API Inference.

<div id="A2.SS3.SSS0.Px1.p1" class="ltx_para">

We set temperature 0.0 and fix the seed 42. Note that there is still non-determinism with fixing the
seed and setting temperature as 0. To reduce the instability, we use the API snapshot form Azure
OpenAI endpoint <span id="A2.SS3.SSS0.Px1.p1.1.1"
class="ltx_text ltx_font_typewriter">gpt-4.1-2025-04-14</span> and <span id="A2.SS3.SSS0.Px1.p1.1.2"
class="ltx_text ltx_font_typewriter">gpt-4.1-mini-2025-04-14</span>.

</div>

</div>

<div id="A2.SS3.SSS0.Px2" class="section ltx_paragraph">

##### Compression.

<div id="A2.SS3.SSS0.Px2.p1" class="ltx_para">

For history compression, we set $`T_{\text{hist}}=4096`$ for AppWorld and OfficeBench, and $`2048`$
for 8-objective QA. We keep the last action, observation pair to preserve the latest information.
This is the same for <span id="A2.SS3.SSS0.Px2.p1.1.1"
class="ltx_text ltx_font_smallcaps">Acon</span> and all baselines. For observation compression, we
set $`T_{\text{obs}}=1024`$ for AppWorld, $`512`$ for OfficeBench, and $`400`$ for 8-objective QA.

</div>

</div>

<div id="A2.SS3.SSS0.Px3" class="section ltx_paragraph">

##### Prompt Optimization.

<div id="A2.SS3.SSS0.Px3.p1" class="ltx_para">

We use the OpenAI <span id="A2.SS3.SSS0.Px3.p1.1.1" class="ltx_text ltx_font_typewriter">o3</span>
model (<a href="#bib.bib8" class="ltx_ref">26</a>) for both analysis and update of prompts. During
the update stage, we sample 5 candidate prompts and select the one that performs best on a subset of
the training set.

</div>

<div id="A2.SS3.SSS0.Px3.p2" class="ltx_para">

The prompts used in each stage and step are provided as follows:

- <span id="A2.I1.i1"><span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_item">•</span></span>
  <div id="A2.I1.i1.p1" class="ltx_para">

  Analysis prompt for <span id="A2.I1.i1.p1.1.1" class="ltx_text" style="--ltx-fg-color:#D2691E;">
  $`\overline{\underline{\textsc{ut}}}`$</span> step:
  Prompt <span class="ltx_ref ltx_missing_label ltx_ref_self"></span>

  </div>
- <span id="A2.I1.i2"><span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_item">•</span></span>
  <div id="A2.I1.i2.p1" class="ltx_para">

  Update prompt for <span id="A2.I1.i2.p1.1.1" class="ltx_text" style="--ltx-fg-color:#D2691E;">
  $`\overline{\underline{\textsc{ut}}}`$</span> step:
  Prompt <span class="ltx_ref ltx_missing_label ltx_ref_self"></span>

  </div>
- <span id="A2.I1.i3"><span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_item">•</span></span>
  <div id="A2.I1.i3.p1" class="ltx_para">

  Analysis prompt for <span id="A2.I1.i3.p1.1.1" class="ltx_text" style="--ltx-fg-color:#4B0082;">
  $`\overline{\underline{\textsc{co}}}`$</span> step:
  Prompt <span class="ltx_ref ltx_missing_label ltx_ref_self"></span>

  </div>
- <span id="A2.I1.i4"><span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_item">•</span></span>
  <div id="A2.I1.i4.p1" class="ltx_para">

  Update prompt for <span id="A2.I1.i4.p1.1.1" class="ltx_text" style="--ltx-fg-color:#4B0082;">
  $`\overline{\underline{\textsc{co}}}`$</span> step:
  Prompt <span class="ltx_ref ltx_missing_label ltx_ref_self"></span>

  </div>

</div>

<div id="A2.SS3.SSS0.Px3.p3" class="ltx_para">

We also provide the detailed procedure in Algorithm <a href="#alg1" class="ltx_ref"
title="Algorithm 1 ‣ Compressed observations. ‣ Appendix D Qualitative Examples ‣ Acon: Optimizing Context Compression for Long-horizon LLM Agents"><span
class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">1</span></a>. For the subset used in prompt selection during
the <span id="A2.SS3.SSS0.Px3.p3.1.1" class="ltx_text" style="--ltx-fg-color:#D2691E;">
$`\overline{\underline{\textsc{ut}}}`$</span> step, we consider training tasks in
$`{\mathcal{D}}_{\sf cont}^{(r)}`$ where the agent succeeds without compression but fails with
compression. For the <span id="A2.SS3.SSS0.Px3.p3.1.2" class="ltx_text"
style="--ltx-fg-color:#4B0082;"> $`\overline{\underline{\textsc{co}}}`$</span> step, we use training
tasks in $`{\mathcal{D}}_{\sf succ}^{(r)}`$ where the agent succeeds with compression. We perform
one round consisting of a single <span id="A2.SS3.SSS0.Px3.p3.1.3" class="ltx_text"
style="--ltx-fg-color:#D2691E;"> $`\overline{\underline{\textsc{ut}}}`$</span> step and a single
<span id="A2.SS3.SSS0.Px3.p3.1.4" class="ltx_text" style="--ltx-fg-color:#4B0082;">
$`\overline{\underline{\textsc{co}}}`$</span> step to obtain the guidelines used in our experiments,
unless otherwise noted.

</div>

</div>

<div id="A2.SS3.SSS0.Px4" class="section ltx_paragraph">

##### Baselines

<div id="A2.SS3.SSS0.Px4.p1" class="ltx_para">

For FIFO, we keep last 5 interaction turns which fits in similar compression rate in average with
<span id="A2.SS3.SSS0.Px4.p1.1.1" class="ltx_text ltx_font_smallcaps">Acon</span>. For retrieval, we
also retrieve 4 interaction turns and keep the last turn. We use OpenAI
<span id="A2.SS3.SSS0.Px4.p1.1.2" class="ltx_text ltx_font_typewriter">text-embedding-3-large</span>
for embedding. For LLMLingua, we set keep rate as 30% for both history and observation. For naive
prompting, we use the similar prompt with <a href="#bib.bib15" class="ltx_ref">20</a> and do some
human prompt engineering to specialize each prompt to history or observation compression.

</div>

</div>

<div id="A2.SS3.SSS0.Px5" class="section ltx_paragraph">

##### Compressor & Agent Distillation

<div id="A2.SS3.SSS0.Px5.p1" class="ltx_para">

Both compressor and agent distillation use LoRA (<a href="#bib.bib9" class="ltx_ref">10</a>) with
rank 16, $`\alpha=32`$, learning rate $`10^{-4}`$, 3 epochs, batch size 4, and maximum sequence
length $`10{,}000`$. We adopt linear warmup ($`5\%`$ ratio), weight decay $`0.01`$, and AdamW
optimizer. No hyperparameter tuning was performed; the same setup is applied across all models and
benchmarks. We sample a single generation from the teacher for fine-tuning, leaving potential
improvements from hyperparameter tuning or multi-sample training for future work. We use 1 A100 80GB
GPU for both training and inference. For inference of fine-tuned models, we use greedy decoding
(temperature 0.0).

</div>

</div>

</div>

</div>

<div id="A3" class="section ltx_appendix">

### <span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_appendix">Appendix C </span>Additional Results

<div id="A3.p1" class="ltx_para">

We provide additional quantitative results to complement the main experiments
in <a href="#S4" class="ltx_ref ltx_refmacro_autoref"
title="4 Experiments ‣ Acon: Optimizing Context Compression for Long-horizon LLM Agents"><span
class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">Section 4</span></a>.

</div>

<div id="A3.SS1" class="section ltx_subsection">

#### <span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_subsection">C.1 </span>Results with different agent models

<div id="A3.SS1.SSS0.Px1" class="section ltx_paragraph">

##### Experiments with gpt-4.1-mini.

<div id="A3.SS1.SSS0.Px1.p1" class="ltx_para">

Results for the smaller variant gpt-4.1-mini (<a href="#bib.bib7" class="ltx_ref">24</a>) across
three benchmarks are reported in AppWorld (<a href="#A4.T9" class="ltx_ref ltx_refmacro_autoref"
title="Table 9 ‣ Compressed observations. ‣ Appendix D Qualitative Examples ‣ Acon: Optimizing Context Compression for Long-horizon LLM Agents"><span
class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">Table 9</span></a>), OfficeBench
(<a href="#A4.T10" class="ltx_ref ltx_refmacro_autoref"
title="Table 10 ‣ Compressed observations. ‣ Appendix D Qualitative Examples ‣ Acon: Optimizing Context Compression for Long-horizon LLM Agents"><span
class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">Table 10</span></a>), and 8-objective QA
(<a href="#A4.T11" class="ltx_ref ltx_refmacro_autoref"
title="Table 11 ‣ Compressed observations. ‣ Appendix D Qualitative Examples ‣ Acon: Optimizing Context Compression for Long-horizon LLM Agents"><span
class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">Table 11</span></a>). The trends of <span id="A3.SS1.SSS0.Px1.p1.1.1"
class="ltx_text ltx_font_smallcaps">Acon</span> are consistent with those for gpt-4.1
in <a href="#S4" class="ltx_ref ltx_refmacro_autoref"
title="4 Experiments ‣ Acon: Optimizing Context Compression for Long-horizon LLM Agents"><span
class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">Section 4</span></a>. In particular,
<a href="#A4.T9" class="ltx_ref ltx_refmacro_autoref"
title="Table 9 ‣ Compressed observations. ‣ Appendix D Qualitative Examples ‣ Acon: Optimizing Context Compression for Long-horizon LLM Agents"><span
class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">Table 9</span></a> shows that history compression improves the
performance of gpt-4.1-mini compared to the baseline, complementing the findings
in <a href="#S4.SS4" class="ltx_ref ltx_refmacro_autoref"
title="4.4 Acon for distilled small agents ‣ 4 Experiments ‣ Acon: Optimizing Context Compression for Long-horizon LLM Agents"><span
class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">Section 4.4</span></a> that <span id="A3.SS1.SSS0.Px1.p1.1.2"
class="ltx_text ltx_font_smallcaps">Acon</span> enhances the effectiveness of smaller LM agents.
These results highlight the robustness of our method under resource-constrained settings.

</div>

</div>

<div id="A3.SS1.SSS0.Px2" class="section ltx_paragraph">

##### Experiments with gpt-5-chat.

<div id="A3.SS1.SSS0.Px2.p1" class="ltx_para">

We also evaluate on AppWorld using gpt-5-chat (<a href="#bib.bib5" class="ltx_ref">25</a>), as
reported in <a href="#A4.T12" class="ltx_ref ltx_refmacro_autoref"
title="Table 12 ‣ Compressed observations. ‣ Appendix D Qualitative Examples ‣ Acon: Optimizing Context Compression for Long-horizon LLM Agents"><span
class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">Table 12</span></a>. The improvements follow the same trend as with
gpt-4.1, demonstrating that <span id="A3.SS1.SSS0.Px2.p1.1.1"
class="ltx_text ltx_font_smallcaps">Acon</span> generalizes to the latest stronger proprietary
models.

</div>

</div>

</div>

<div id="A3.SS2" class="section ltx_subsection">

#### <span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_subsection">C.2 </span>Detailed results and analyses

<div id="A3.SS2.SSS0.Px1" class="section ltx_paragraph">

##### OfficeBench difficulty breakdowns.

<div id="A3.SS2.SSS0.Px1.p1" class="ltx_para">

We further analyze OfficeBench with gpt-4.1 by difficulty level. The detailed breakdown
in <a href="#A4.T8" class="ltx_ref ltx_refmacro_autoref"
title="Table 8 ‣ Compressed observations. ‣ Appendix D Qualitative Examples ‣ Acon: Optimizing Context Compression for Long-horizon LLM Agents"><span
class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">Table 8</span></a> shows that <span id="A3.SS2.SSS0.Px1.p1.1.1"
class="ltx_text ltx_font_smallcaps">Acon</span> yields the largest gains on the most challenging
tasks in Level 3.

</div>

</div>

<div id="A3.SS2.SSS0.Px2" class="section ltx_paragraph">

##### Distilled optimizer.

<div id="A3.SS2.SSS0.Px2.p1" class="ltx_para">

Additional results for the distilled optimizer in AppWorld are shown
in <a href="#A4.T13" class="ltx_ref ltx_refmacro_autoref"
title="Table 13 ‣ Compressed observations. ‣ Appendix D Qualitative Examples ‣ Acon: Optimizing Context Compression for Long-horizon LLM Agents"><span
class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">Table 13</span></a>. Beyond the analysis
in <a href="#S4.SS3" class="ltx_ref ltx_refmacro_autoref"
title="4.3 Compressor distillation ‣ 4 Experiments ‣ Acon: Optimizing Context Compression for Long-horizon LLM Agents"><span
class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">Section 4.3</span></a>, we also include experiments where the
compressor is distilled using guidelines without optimization. The results confirm that optimized
guidelines consistently yield stronger performance when distilled into smaller models.

</div>

</div>

<div id="A3.SS2.SSS0.Px3" class="section ltx_paragraph">

##### History and observation compression.

<div id="A3.SS2.SSS0.Px3.p1" class="ltx_para">

In <a href="#A4.T14" class="ltx_ref ltx_refmacro_autoref"
title="Table 14 ‣ Compressed observations. ‣ Appendix D Qualitative Examples ‣ Acon: Optimizing Context Compression for Long-horizon LLM Agents"><span
class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">Table 14</span></a>, we report ablations with gpt-4.1 using both
history and observation compression. While combining the two compressions achieves larger reductions
in peak token usage and dependency, it also leads to substantial performance degradation compared to
applying either compression alone.

</div>

</div>

<div id="A3.SS2.SSS0.Px4" class="section ltx_paragraph">

##### Additional guideline optimization step.

<div id="A3.SS2.SSS0.Px4.p1" class="ltx_para">

We investigate whether running an extra utility maximization step (<span id="A3.SS2.SSS0.Px4.p1.1.1"
class="ltx_text" style="--ltx-fg-color:#D2691E;"> $`\overline{\underline{\textsc{ut}}}`$</span>)
after the standard sequence of utility maximization and compression maximization
(<span id="A3.SS2.SSS0.Px4.p1.1.2" class="ltx_text" style="--ltx-fg-color:#4B0082;">
$`\overline{\underline{\textsc{co}}}`$</span>) is beneficial. As shown
in <a href="#A4.T14" class="ltx_ref ltx_refmacro_autoref"
title="Table 14 ‣ Compressed observations. ‣ Appendix D Qualitative Examples ‣ Acon: Optimizing Context Compression for Long-horizon LLM Agents"><span
class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">Table 14</span></a>, this additional iteration results in a performance
drop, indicating that a single round of optimization is sufficient for effective guideline learning.

</div>

</div>

<div id="A3.SS2.SSS0.Px5" class="section ltx_paragraph">

##### Distilled compressor for observation.

<div id="A3.SS2.SSS0.Px5.p1" class="ltx_para">

In addition to <a href="#S4.SS3" class="ltx_ref ltx_refmacro_autoref"
title="4.3 Compressor distillation ‣ 4 Experiments ‣ Acon: Optimizing Context Compression for Long-horizon LLM Agents"><span
class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">Section 4.3</span></a>, we report results for observation compressor
distillation in <a href="#A4.F7" class="ltx_ref ltx_refmacro_autoref"
title="Figure 7 ‣ Compressed observations. ‣ Appendix D Qualitative Examples ‣ Acon: Optimizing Context Compression for Long-horizon LLM Agents"><span
class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">Figure 7</span></a>. Similar to history compression, the performance is
largely preserved after distillation, confirming that <span id="A3.SS2.SSS0.Px5.p1.1.1"
class="ltx_text ltx_font_smallcaps">Acon</span> enables effective transfer of optimized observation
compressors to smaller models.

</div>

</div>

<div id="A3.SS2.SSS0.Px6" class="section ltx_paragraph">

##### API cost details on compression guideline optimization.

<div id="A3.SS2.SSS0.Px6.p1" class="ltx_para">

In this section, we provide a detailed breakdown of the computational costs associated with our
framework. All cost estimates are based on the official API pricing as
in <a href="#A2.SS2" class="ltx_ref ltx_refmacro_autoref"
title="B.2 Evaluation Metrics ‣ Appendix B Experimental Setup Details ‣ Acon: Optimizing Context Compression for Long-horizon LLM Agents"><span
class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">Section B.2</span></a>. The total expense is categorized into two
phases: trajectory rollout and guideline optimization. <span id="A3.SS2.SSS0.Px6.p1.1.1"
class="ltx_text ltx_font_italic">(1) Trajectory Rollout (Data Collection).</span> This phase
accounts for the majority of the budget. We utilize <span id="A3.SS2.SSS0.Px6.p1.1.2"
class="ltx_text ltx_font_typewriter">gpt-4.1</span> to collect trajectories on the training set
(<span id="A3.SS2.SSS0.Px6.p1.1.3" class="ltx_text ltx_font_italic">e.g.,</span> 90 examples for
AppWorld). For each example, we generate two trajectories: one without compression and one with
compressed context. The total cost for collecting these rollout trajectories amounts to
approximately \$20. It is important to note that this is a one-time data preparation cost which can
be amortized across future agent runs or substituted with existing offline logs.
<span id="A3.SS2.SSS0.Px6.p1.1.4" class="ltx_text ltx_font_italic">(2) Guideline
Optimization.</span> Despite utilizing the reasoning-intensive <span id="A3.SS2.SSS0.Px6.p1.1.5"
class="ltx_text ltx_font_typewriter">o3</span> model, the optimization process is highly
cost-efficient. The procedure runs for a single number of iteration. In each iteration, the
optimizer generates 5 candidate guidelines and performs one <span id="A3.SS2.SSS0.Px6.p1.1.6"
class="ltx_text" style="--ltx-fg-color:#D2691E;"> $`\overline{\underline{\textsc{ut}}}`$</span> step
and one <span id="A3.SS2.SSS0.Px6.p1.1.7" class="ltx_text" style="--ltx-fg-color:#4B0082;">
$`\overline{\underline{\textsc{co}}}`$</span> step. The total cost for the entire optimization loop
is consistently under \$2 per domain.

</div>

</div>

<div id="A3.SS2.SSS0.Px7" class="section ltx_paragraph">

##### Additional Results on WebVoyager

<div id="A3.SS2.SSS0.Px7.p1" class="ltx_para">

To further evaluate ACON beyond simulated productivity and question-answering environments, we
conduct additional experiments on WebVoyager (<a href="#bib.bib60" class="ltx_ref">9</a>), a
web-agent benchmark that requires agents to interact with webpages and complete user-specified
tasks. We use 50 training tasks for compression guideline optimization and evaluate the resulting
guidelines on 70 held-out test tasks. We evaluate WebVoyager using gpt-4.1 as both the agent and
compressor, following the main experimental setting.

</div>

<figure id="A3.T6" class="ltx_table ltx_align_floatright" style="width:32%;">

(<span class="math inline">10<sup>3</sup></span>), and dependency (<span
class="math inline">10<sup>6</sup></span>).</span></figcaption>
</figure>

<div id="A3.SS2.SSS0.Px7.p2" class="ltx_para">

This setting introduces different context dynamics from AppWorld, OfficeBench, and 8-objective QA,
as the agent must process verbose webpage observations represented as accessibility trees (AXTrees)
while maintaining relevant interaction history across multiple
steps (<a href="#bib.bib15" class="ltx_ref">20</a>).

</div>

<div id="A3.SS2.SSS0.Px7.p3" class="ltx_para">

As reported in Table <a href="#A3.T6" class="ltx_ref"
title="Table 6 ‣ Additional Results on WebVoyager ‣ C.2 Detailed results and analyses ‣ Appendix C Additional Results ‣ Acon: Optimizing Context Compression for Long-horizon LLM Agents"><span
class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">6</span></a>, <span id="A3.SS2.SSS0.Px7.p3.1.1"
class="ltx_text ltx_font_smallcaps">Acon</span> improves task accuracy over both no compression and
prompting baselines while substantially reducing peak tokens and dependency compared to no
compression. For history compression, <span id="A3.SS2.SSS0.Px7.p3.1.2"
class="ltx_text ltx_font_smallcaps">Acon</span> improves accuracy from 42.9 to 48.6 compared to
prompting, with comparable peak tokens and lower dependency. For observation compression,
<span id="A3.SS2.SSS0.Px7.p3.1.3" class="ltx_text ltx_font_smallcaps">Acon</span> improves accuracy
from 45.7 to 47.1 and reduces dependency from 0.85 to 0.80. These results suggest that the optimized
compression guidelines learned by <span id="A3.SS2.SSS0.Px7.p3.1.4"
class="ltx_text ltx_font_smallcaps">Acon</span> are effective not only in structured tool-use
environments, but also in web-agent settings where observations are verbose and dynamically
changing.

</div>

</div>

<div id="A3.SS2.SSS0.Px8" class="section ltx_paragraph">

##### Case study: history compression turns failure into success.

<div id="A3.SS2.SSS0.Px8.p1" class="ltx_para">

A notable case study illustrates how history compression enables a smaller agent to succeed on tasks
that would otherwise fail. In the uncompressed trajectory in
Example <a href="#A4.SS0.SSS0.Px3" class="ltx_ref"
title="Compressed observations. ‣ Appendix D Qualitative Examples ‣ Acon: Optimizing Context Compression for Long-horizon LLM Agents"><span
class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">D</span></a>, the gpt-4.1-mini agent repeatedly attempted to use the
<span id="A3.SS2.SSS0.Px8.p1.1.1" class="ltx_text ltx_font_typewriter">file_system</span> APIs
without managing authentication, leading to persistent 401 Unauthorized errors. After compressing
the history as in Example <a href="#A4.SS0.SSS0.Px3" class="ltx_ref"
title="Compressed observations. ‣ Appendix D Qualitative Examples ‣ Acon: Optimizing Context Compression for Long-horizon LLM Agents"><span
class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">D</span></a>, however, the compressed history retained only the
essential reasoning steps: the need for both username and password, the importance of passing the
returned <span id="A3.SS2.SSS0.Px8.p1.1.2" class="ltx_text ltx_font_typewriter">access_token</span>
into subsequent calls, and the absence of proxy APIs in the supervisor app.

</div>

<div id="A3.SS2.SSS0.Px8.p2" class="ltx_para">

This compressed context prevented redundant exploration and guided the agent directly to the correct
sequence—login with full credentials, capture the token, and provide it explicitly in
<span id="A3.SS2.SSS0.Px8.p2.1.1" class="ltx_text ltx_font_typewriter">show_directory</span> and
<span id="A3.SS2.SSS0.Px8.p2.1.2" class="ltx_text ltx_font_typewriter">delete_file</span> calls. As
a result, the agent was able to enumerate and remove all.pdf files in  /downloads, a task it had
previously failed. This example highlights how compression does not merely shorten history but
clarifies critical dependencies, turning a failure trajectory into a successful one.

</div>

<figcaption><span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_table"><span id="A3.T7.4" class="ltx_text"
style="font-size:90%;">Table 7</span>: </span><span id="A3.T7.5" class="ltx_text"
style="font-size:90%;">Comparison between <span id="A3.T7.5.1"
class="ltx_text ltx_font_smallcaps">Acon</span> and MEM1-like methods. The two methods operate under
different assumptions regarding model accessibility, training requirements, and architectural
coupling.</span></figcaption>
</figure>

</div>

</div>

<div id="A3.SS3" class="section ltx_subsection">

#### <span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_subsection">C.3 </span>Comparison with MEM1

<div id="A3.SS3.p1" class="ltx_para">

MEM1 (<a href="#bib.bib28" class="ltx_ref">61</a>) and concurrent
works (<a href="#bib.bib58" class="ltx_ref">36</a>; <a href="#bib.bib56" class="ltx_ref">55</a>)
propose a learnable context compression policy trained jointly with the agent through reinforcement
learning. This design couples reasoning and compression within a single trainable model and requires
full access to model weights and gradient updates. In contrast, our method can perform optimization
entirely at the prompt-level without any weight updates and enables the agent and compressor to be
different models.

</div>

<div id="A3.SS3.p2" class="ltx_para">

This decoupling allows combinations that are not possible in MEM1 and other similar methods. For
example, one can use a large proprietary model such as gpt-4.1 as the agent while employing a
smaller open-source model such as Qwen3-14B as the compressor after distillation, a configuration
that other MEM1-like methods cannot support due to its unified training requirement. This
flexibility makes <span id="A3.SS3.p2.1.1" class="ltx_text ltx_font_smallcaps">Acon</span>
applicable to both open-source and proprietary API-based models, including settings where model
weights are inaccessible. A detailed comparison is summarized
in <a href="#A3.T7" class="ltx_ref ltx_refmacro_autoref"
title="Table 7 ‣ Case study: history compression turns failure into success. ‣ C.2 Detailed results and analyses ‣ Appendix C Additional Results ‣ Acon: Optimizing Context Compression for Long-horizon LLM Agents"><span
class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">Table 7</span></a>.

</div>

</div>

</div>

<div id="A4" class="section ltx_appendix">

### <span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_appendix">Appendix D </span>Qualitative Examples

<div id="A4.p1" class="ltx_para">

We complement the quantitative results with qualitative illustrations.

</div>

<div id="A4.SS0.SSS0.Px1" class="section ltx_paragraph">

##### Compression guidelines.

<div id="A4.SS0.SSS0.Px1.p1" class="ltx_para">

We present examples of compression guidelines before and after optimization in AppWorld. The history
compression guideline before optimization is shown in
Prompt <span class="ltx_ref ltx_missing_label ltx_ref_self"></span>, the optimized version
(<span id="A4.SS0.SSS0.Px1.p1.1.1" class="ltx_text" style="--ltx-fg-color:#D2691E;">
$`\overline{\underline{\textsc{ut}}}`$</span>) in
Prompt <span class="ltx_ref ltx_missing_label ltx_ref_self"></span>, and the optimized version
(<span id="A4.SS0.SSS0.Px1.p1.1.2" class="ltx_text" style="--ltx-fg-color:#D2691E;">
$`\overline{\underline{\textsc{ut}}}`$</span> <span id="A4.SS0.SSS0.Px1.p1.1.3" class="ltx_text"
style="--ltx-fg-color:#4B0082;"> $`\overline{\underline{\textsc{co}}}`$</span>) in
Prompt <span class="ltx_ref ltx_missing_label ltx_ref_self"></span>. Similarly, observation
compression guideline examples are provided in
Prompt <span class="ltx_ref ltx_missing_label ltx_ref_self"></span> and
Prompt <span class="ltx_ref ltx_missing_label ltx_ref_self"></span>, and the optimized version
(<span id="A4.SS0.SSS0.Px1.p1.1.4" class="ltx_text" style="--ltx-fg-color:#D2691E;">
$`\overline{\underline{\textsc{ut}}}`$</span> <span id="A4.SS0.SSS0.Px1.p1.1.5" class="ltx_text"
style="--ltx-fg-color:#4B0082;"> $`\overline{\underline{\textsc{co}}}`$</span>) in
Prompt <span class="ltx_ref ltx_missing_label ltx_ref_self"></span>. These comparisons demonstrate
that optimization yields more targeted guidelines for compressors.

</div>

</div>

<div id="A4.SS0.SSS0.Px2" class="section ltx_paragraph">

##### Compressed histories.

<div id="A4.SS0.SSS0.Px2.p1" class="ltx_para">

Compression Example <span class="ltx_ref ltx_missing_label ltx_ref_self"></span> illustrates history
segments before and after guideline optimization in AppWorld with gpt-4.1. The optimized guideline
retains a more detailed record of task progress, including variable states and guardrails for the
environment. After the compression maximization step (<span id="A4.SS0.SSS0.Px2.p1.1.1"
class="ltx_text" style="--ltx-fg-color:#4B0082;"> $`\overline{\underline{\textsc{co}}}`$</span>),
the histories become shorter while still preserving the essential information required for future
decision-making. This qualitative evidence demonstrates how our framework improves both the
efficiency and effectiveness of context compression, complementing the guideline optimization
procedure described in <a href="#S3.SS3" class="ltx_ref ltx_refmacro_autoref"
title="3.3 Optimizing Compression Guidelines ‣ 3 Agent Context Optimization (Acon) ‣ Acon: Optimizing Context Compression for Long-horizon LLM Agents"><span
class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">Section 3.3</span></a>.

</div>

<div id="A4.SS0.SSS0.Px2.p2" class="ltx_para">

We also present Compression Example <span class="ltx_ref ltx_missing_label ltx_ref_self"></span> for
8-objective QA and Compression Example <span class="ltx_ref ltx_missing_label ltx_ref_self"></span>
for OfficeBench, which confirm that the effects of guideline optimization are consistent across
benchmarks.

</div>

</div>

<div id="A4.SS0.SSS0.Px3" class="section ltx_paragraph">

##### Compressed observations.

<div id="A4.SS0.SSS0.Px3.p1" class="ltx_para">

Compression Example <span class="ltx_ref ltx_missing_label ltx_ref_self"></span> shows observations
before and after guideline optimization in AppWorld. We illustrate the case of printing available
APIs for the Spotify app, which produces a lengthy observation. The optimized guideline yields a
more structured and faithful representation: whereas naive prompting loses the JSON format and omits
the crucial “play_music” API, the optimized version preserves both structure and key functionality
necessary to complete the task.

</div>

<div class="ltx_pagination ltx_role_newpage">

</div>

class="math inline">𝒟<sub>succ</sub><sup>(<em>r</em>)</sup> ← {<strong>H</strong><sub><em>i</em></sub><sup>′</sup> ∣ <em>r</em><sub><em>i</em></sub> = 1}</span>
</div>
<div id="algx1.l34" class="ltx_listingline">
<span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_listingline"><span id="algx1.l34.1" class="ltx_text"
style="font-size:80%;">34:</span></span>   <span id="algx1.l34.3" class="ltx_text ltx_font_bold">for
all</span> <span
class="math inline"><strong>H</strong><sup>′</sup> ∈ 𝒟<sub>succ</sub><sup>(<em>r</em>)</sup></span>
<span id="algx1.l34.4" class="ltx_text ltx_font_bold">do</span>
</div>

<span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_listingline"><span id="algx1.l35.1" class="ltx_text"

</div>

</div>

<span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_listingline"><span id="algx1.l37.1" class="ltx_text"

multiset <span class="math inline">ℱ<sub>comp</sub></span>
</div>

style="font-size:80%;">39:</span></span>   <span
class="math inline">$\{\tilde{{\mathcal{P}}}^{(r+2)}_{k}\}_{k=1}^{K}\leftarrow\mathrm{LLM}(\mathrm{UpdateInstr},{\mathcal{P}}_{\mathrm{util}}^{(r+1)},\|_{f\in\mathcal{F}_{\mathrm{comp}}}f)$</span>
</div>

Select by reward-cost:</span> evaluate on a held-out split of <span class="math inline">ℐ</span> and
pick

class="math inline">$k^{*}_{\mathrm{comp}}\leftarrow\arg\max_{k}\Big(\mathrm{SuccessRate}(\{r_{i}(\tilde{{\mathcal{P}}}^{(r+2)}_{k}))\})-\lambda\cdot\mathrm{NormCost}(\{C({\bm{H}}^{\prime}_{i}(\tilde{{\mathcal{P}}}^{(r+2)}_{k}))\})\Big)$</span>
</div>

<figcaption><span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_table"><span id="A4.T9.5" class="ltx_text"
style="font-size:90%;">Table 9</span>: </span><span id="A4.T9.6" class="ltx_text"
style="font-size:90%;">Results across different difficulty levels on <span id="A4.T9.6.1"
class="ltx_text ltx_font_bold">Appworld</span> benchmark (test-normal) with gpt-4.1-mini. We adopt
the same compression guidelines as those used in the gpt-4.1 experiments. Each block reports
accuracy (task goal completion score), average steps, average peak input tokens (<span
class="math inline">10<sup>3</sup></span>), and average dependency (<span
class="math inline">10<sup>6</sup></span>) for agents. Best results in each column are highlighted
in bold. Rows in blue background indicate the results from <span id="A4.T9.6.2"
class="ltx_text ltx_font_bold" style="--ltx-bg-color:#E6F5FF;">ours</span>.</span></figcaption>
</figure>

class="ltx_text ltx_font_bold">OfficeBench</span> benchmark with gpt-4.1-mini. We adopt the same
compression guidelines as those used in the gpt-4.1 experiments. We report accuracy (%), and
efficiency metrics: average steps, peak input tokens (<span
class="math inline">10<sup>3</sup></span>), and dependency (<span
class="math inline">10<sup>6</sup></span>) for Average and each difficulty level. Rows in blue
background indicate the results from <span id="A4.T10.6.2" class="ltx_text ltx_font_bold"
style="--ltx-bg-color:#E6F5FF;">ours</span>.</span></figcaption>
</figure>

<figcaption><span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_table"><span id="A4.T12.5" class="ltx_text"
style="font-size:90%;">Table 12</span>: </span><span id="A4.T12.6" class="ltx_text"
style="font-size:90%;">Results across different difficulty levels on <span id="A4.T12.6.1"
class="ltx_text ltx_font_bold">AppWorld</span> benchmark (test-normal) with gpt-5-chat. We adopt the
same compression guidelines as those used in the gpt-4.1 experiments. Each block reports accuracy
(task goal completion score), steps, peak input tokens (<span
class="math inline">10<sup>3</sup></span>), and dependency (<span
class="math inline">10<sup>6</sup></span>) for agents. Best results in each column are highlighted
in bold. Rows in blue background indicate the results from <span id="A4.T12.6.2"
class="ltx_text ltx_font_bold" style="--ltx-bg-color:#E6F5FF;">ours</span>.</span></figcaption>
</figure>

<figcaption><span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_table"><span id="A4.T13.6" class="ltx_text"
style="font-size:90%;">Table 13</span>: </span><span id="A4.T13.7" class="ltx_text"
style="font-size:90%;">Results across different difficulty levels on <span id="A4.T13.7.1"
class="ltx_text ltx_font_bold">AppWorld</span> with <span id="A4.T13.7.2"
class="ltx_text ltx_font_bold">distilled compressors</span>. We report accuracy (task goal
completion score), average steps, peak input tokens (<span
class="math inline">10<sup>3</sup></span>), and dependency (<span
class="math inline">10<sup>6</sup></span>). For all compressors, we use the optimized compression
guideline after the utilization maximization <span id="A4.T13.7.3" class="ltx_text"
style="--ltx-fg-color:#D2691E;"> <span
class="math inline">$\overline{\underline{\textsc{ut}}}$</span></span> step. ‘Fine-tune’ means that
we fine-tune small models with outputs from naive prompt before compression guideline
optimization.</span></figcaption>
</figure>

<figcaption><span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_table"><span id="A4.T14.5" class="ltx_text"
style="font-size:90%;">Table 14</span>: </span><span id="A4.T14.6" class="ltx_text"
style="font-size:90%;">Additional results for additional guideline optimization step and unified
compression on <span id="A4.T14.6.1" class="ltx_text ltx_font_bold">Appworld</span> benchmark
(test-normal). Each block reports accuracy (task goal completion score), steps, peak input tokens
(<span class="math inline">10<sup>3</sup></span>), and dependency (<span
class="math inline">10<sup>6</sup></span>) for agents. Best results in each column are highlighted
in bold. Rows in blue background indicate the results from <span id="A4.T14.6.2"
class="ltx_text ltx_font_bold" style="--ltx-bg-color:#E6F5FF;">ours</span>.</span></figcaption>
</figure>

<figure id="A4.F7" class="ltx_figure">

<figcaption><span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_figure"><span id="A4.F7.5" class="ltx_text"
style="font-size:90%;">Figure 7</span>: </span><span id="A4.F7.6" class="ltx_text ltx_font_bold"
style="font-size:90%;">Results of distilled compressors on observation compression<span
id="A4.F7.6.1" class="ltx_text ltx_font_medium"> with gpt-4.1 as the agent. Student models
(Qwen3-14B, Qwen3-8B, Phi-4) are distilled from gpt-4.1 compressor using the optimized compression
guideline after <span id="A4.F7.6.1.1" class="ltx_text" style="--ltx-fg-color:#D2691E;"> <span
class="math inline">$\overline{\underline{\textsc{ut}}}$</span></span> step, and evaluated across
all benchmarks. We also include result with gpt-4.1-mini without distillation for
comparison.</span></span></figcaption>
</figure>

<div class="ltx_pagination ltx_role_newpage">

</div>

<div id="A4.SS0.SSS0.Px3.p2" class="ltx_para">

uto counter, number within=section\]promptbox\[2\]\[\] colback=white, colframe=blue!80!black,
coltitle=white, title=Prompt <span id="A4.SS0.SSS0.Px3.p2.1.1"
class="ltx_ERROR undefined">\thetcbcounter</span>: \#2, fonttitle=<span id="A4.SS0.SSS0.Px3.p2.1.2"
class="ltx_text ltx_font_bold">, boxrule=1pt, arc=2mm, top=2mm, bottom=2mm, width=0.95breakable,
listing only, listing engine=listings, listing options=
basicstyle=<span id="A4.SS0.SSS0.Px3.p2.1.2.1" class="ltx_text ltx_font_typewriter"
style="font-size:90%;">, breaklines=true, columns=fullflexible, keepspaces=true </span>, \#1 </span>
<span id="A4.SS0.SSS0.Px3.p2.1.3"
class="ltx_ERROR undefined">{promptbox}</span>\[label=box:prompt_optimizer_analysis\]Prompt for
analysis before prompt optimization (utility step) You are an expert agent trajectory auditor.

</div>

<div id="A4.SS0.SSS0.Px3.p3" class="ltx_para">

Analyze why the HISTORY-OPTIMIZED agent failed OR became significantly less efficient while the
BASELINE succeeded. You are given: - task_name: task_name - Baseline full history (single continuous
session) - Optimized history split into multiple sessions where each new session starts with a fresh
system + user prompt and an injected \<HISTORY_SUMMARY\> summarizing earlier interactions. -
baseline_success= baseline_success optimized_success= optimized_success - baseline_env_steps=
baseline_env_steps \| default(’null’) optimized_env_steps= optimized_env_steps \| default(’null’)
step_ratio= step_ratio \| default(’null’) performance_regression= performance_regression \|
default(’false’)

</div>

<div id="A4.SS0.SSS0.Px3.p4" class="ltx_para">

Goals: 1. Determine whether summarization / session resetting removed, distorted, delayed, or
bloated reasoning causing failure OR inflated step count (\> threshold factor of baseline). 2.
Identify the FIRST divergence point where the optimized trajectory meaningfully deviates from the
successful efficient baseline path. 3. Categorize root causes (e.g., Missing Critical Fact,
Incorrect Summary, Lost Variable/State, Unnecessary Re-discovery, Instruction Drift, API Misuse,
Premature Completion, Token Truncation, Inefficient Looping, Redundant API Calls, Over-Exploration,
Other). 4. Extract concrete evidence snippets (quote exact lines) from baseline vs optimized
showing: - Critical facts present in baseline but absent/altered in optimized (esp. after a session
boundary) - Summary inaccuracies (baseline ground truth vs summary text) - Redundant or looping
action patterns causing step inflation. 5. Suggest precise remediation strategies: summary style
changes, retain variable/value tables, move session boundaries, guardrail prompts, caching,
early-exit heuristics, loop detection, etc. 6. Provide a reliability_score (0.0-1.0) reflecting
confidence in your causal attribution. 7. If performance_regression==true, analyze efficiency
degradation even if optimized_success==true.

</div>

<div id="A4.SS0.SSS0.Px3.p5" class="ltx_para">

Output STRICTLY valid JSON object with keys: "task_name": str, "divergence_step_description": str,
"root_cause_categories": \[str, …\], "missing_or_distorted_facts": \[ "baseline": str,
"optimized_context_absent_or_changed": str, "impact": str \], "summary_inaccuracies": \[
"summary_excerpt": str, "issue_type": str, "correct_baseline_reference": str, "impact": str \],
"lost_state_variables": \[ "name_or_pattern": str, "baseline_evidence": str, "optimized_issue": str
\], "api_or_action_errors": \[ "optimized_step_excerpt": str, "error_type": str, "improvement": str
\], "inefficiency_patterns": \[ "pattern": str, "evidence_excerpt": str, "excess_steps": int,
"cause": str, "remediation": str \], "timeline_of_divergence": \[ "phase": str, "optimized_excerpt":
str, "baseline_contrast": str, "effect": str \], "performance_regression": bool,
"baseline_env_steps": int \| null, "optimized_env_steps": int \| null, "step_ratio": float \| null,
"remediation_recommendations": \[ str, … \], "recovery_opportunities_missed": \[
"optimized_excerpt": str, "missed_fix_action": str \], "reliability_score": float,
"concise_failure_mechanism_summary": str

</div>

<div id="A4.SS0.SSS0.Px3.p6" class="ltx_para">

If some sections have no data, use an empty list. For non-applicable numeric fields use null. Do NOT
include any extra commentary outside JSON.

</div>

<div id="A4.SS0.SSS0.Px3.p7" class="ltx_para">

— BASELINE_HISTORY_START baseline_history BASELINE_HISTORY_END

</div>

<div id="A4.SS0.SSS0.Px3.p8" class="ltx_para">

OPTIMIZED_MULTI_SESSION_HISTORY_START optimized_history OPTIMIZED_MULTI_SESSION_HISTORY_END

</div>

<div id="A4.SS0.SSS0.Px3.p9" class="ltx_para">

Failure or performance report / metadata (may be null): failure_report

</div>

<div id="A4.SS0.SSS0.Px3.p10" class="ltx_para">

Proceed with rigorous comparison. <span id="A4.SS0.SSS0.Px3.p10.1.1"
class="ltx_ERROR undefined">{promptbox}</span>\[label=box:prompt_optimizer_update\]Prompt for prompt
optimization after analysis (utility step) You are an expert prompt engineer tasked with refining a
HISTORY SUMMARIZATION prompt. Rewrite the ORIGINAL PROMPT to reduce length of the HISTORY SUMMARY
while preserving factual continuity for the next session. Ground all changes in the PER-SAMPLE
REDUCTION SIGNALS below. Do not aggregate across samples; use the patterns and rules as-is.

</div>

<div id="A4.SS0.SSS0.Px3.p11" class="ltx_para">

Constraints: - Keep all Jinja placeholders, variable names, and structure intact where possible. -
Add explicit, concrete rules that prevent verbosity and retain essential state. - Do not include
literal values from prior content; refer to variable names only. - Output ONLY the improved prompt
template (no extra commentary).

</div>

<div id="A4.SS0.SSS0.Px3.p12" class="ltx_para">

Context (samples below are the only ground truth signals to use): - Average original summary size
(chars) across sampled set: avg_orig_chars

<span id="A4.SS0.SSS0.Px3.p35.1" class="ltx_ERROR undefined">{promptbox}</span>

\[label=box:appworld_history_compression\]AppWorld Prompt for history compression before
optimization You are maintaining a structured context-aware summary for a productivity agent. You
will be given the user instruction for the agent, a list of interactions corresponding to actions
taken by the agent, and the most recent previous summary if one exists. Produce the following:

</div>

<div id="A4.SS0.SSS0.Px3.p36" class="ltx_para">

\### REASONING Summarize key progress, decisions made, important observed outcomes, and rationale
behind actions taken so far. Include how earlier steps influenced later ones and why certain data is
retained in the summary.

</div>

<div id="A4.SS0.SSS0.Px3.p37" class="ltx_para">

\### COMPLETED List completed subtasks or successful outcomes, with brief results if applicable.

</div>

<div id="A4.SS0.SSS0.Px3.p38" class="ltx_para">

—

</div>

<div id="A4.SS0.SSS0.Px3.p39" class="ltx_para">

\## \[Information Source\]

</div>

<div id="A4.SS0.SSS0.Px3.p40" class="ltx_para">

\### USER INSTRUCTION

</div>

<div id="A4.SS0.SSS0.Px3.p41" class="ltx_para">

task

</div>

<div id="A4.SS0.SSS0.Px3.p42" class="ltx_para">

\## \[PREVIOUS SUMMARY\] (if any)

</div>

<div id="A4.SS0.SSS0.Px3.p43" class="ltx_para">

prev_summary

</div>

<div id="A4.SS0.SSS0.Px3.p44" class="ltx_para">

\## \[HISTORY OF INTERACTIONS\]

</div>

<div id="A4.SS0.SSS0.Px3.p45" class="ltx_para">

history

</div>

<div id="A4.SS0.SSS0.Px3.p46" class="ltx_para">

—

</div>

<div id="A4.SS0.SSS0.Px3.p47" class="ltx_para">

\## PRIORITIZE

</div>

<div id="A4.SS0.SSS0.Px3.p48" class="ltx_para">

1\. Keep all sections relevant and concise. 2. Use reusable structured formats when summarizing
artifacts. 3. Ensure agent can resume task with no loss of information. 4. Include key info from
errors or failed attempts to prevent repeated mistakes. 5. Preserve all essential artifacts and data
needed to complete the task.

</div>

<div id="A4.SS0.SSS0.Px3.p49" class="ltx_para">

—

</div>

<div id="A4.SS0.SSS0.Px3.p50" class="ltx_para">

\### \[Output Format\]

</div>

<div id="A4.SS0.SSS0.Px3.p51" class="ltx_para">

Do \*\*not\*\* include the input or any additional explanation. Only return the formatted summary.
<span id="A4.SS0.SSS0.Px3.p51.1.1"
class="ltx_ERROR undefined">{promptbox}</span>\[label=box:appworld_history_optimized\]AppWorld
Prompt for history compression after optimization (<span id="A4.SS0.SSS0.Px3.p51.1.2"
class="ltx_text" style="--ltx-fg-color:#D2691E;"> $`\overline{\underline{\textsc{ut}}}`$</span>) You
maintain a compact, state-preserving HISTORY_SUMMARY for a multi-session agent.

</div>

<div id="A4.SS0.SSS0.Px3.p52" class="ltx_para">

Input: \[USER INSTRUCTION\] task \[PREVIOUS SUMMARY\] prev_summary \[HISTORY OF INTERACTIONS\]
history

</div>

<div id="A4.SS0.SSS0.Px3.p53" class="ltx_para">

Create the following sections-use the exact headings and order:

</div>

<div id="A4.SS0.SSS0.Px3.p54" class="ltx_para">

\<HISTORY_SUMMARY\>

</div>

<div id="A4.SS0.SSS0.Px3.p55" class="ltx_para">

1\. REASONING - Key progress, decisions, outcomes, and their rationale. - Note how earlier steps
influence later ones.

</div>

<div id="A4.SS0.SSS0.Px3.p56" class="ltx_para">

2\. VARS \| name \| value \| purpose \| \|——\|——-\|———\| Record every runtime value the next session
must re-declare (tokens, ids, lists, last page_index/page_limit, etc.).

</div>

<div id="A4.SS0.SSS0.Px3.p57" class="ltx_para">

3\. TODO List pending actions with enough detail to execute directly.

</div>

<div id="A4.SS0.SSS0.Px3.p58" class="ltx_para">

4\. COMPLETED Bullet list of finished subtasks with brief results.

</div>

<div id="A4.SS0.SSS0.Px3.p59" class="ltx_para">

5\. GUARDRAILS Short reminders that prevent repeat errors, e.g. - Memory resets; re-create VARS
before use. - Paginate until empty page. - Validate API parameters against spec. - Avoid redundant
logins or doc look-ups.

</div>

<div id="A4.SS0.SSS0.Px3.p60" class="ltx_para">

Requirements: - Be concise-bullets and tables preferred; no extraneous prose. - Preserve all
essential facts, parameters, and artifacts; omit nothing critical. - Include errors only if they
inform future avoidance. - Do not output the input or any commentary-return only
\<HISTORY_SUMMARY\>. <span id="A4.SS0.SSS0.Px3.p60.1.1"
class="ltx_ERROR undefined">{promptbox}</span>\[label=box:appworld_history_optimized_len\]AppWorld
Prompt for history compression after optimization\
(<span id="A4.SS0.SSS0.Px3.p60.1.2" class="ltx_text" style="--ltx-fg-color:#D2691E;">
$`\overline{\underline{\textsc{ut}}}`$</span> <span id="A4.SS0.SSS0.Px3.p60.1.3" class="ltx_text"
style="--ltx-fg-color:#4B0082;"> $`\overline{\underline{\textsc{co}}}`$</span>) You maintain a
compact, state-preserving HISTORY_SUMMARY for a multi-session agent.

</div>

<div id="A4.SS0.SSS0.Px3.p61" class="ltx_para">

Input: \[USER INSTRUCTION\] task \[PREVIOUS SUMMARY\] prev_summary \[HISTORY OF INTERACTIONS\]
history

</div>

<div id="A4.SS0.SSS0.Px3.p62" class="ltx_para">

Summary Compression Rules: - Collapse multi-bullet narratives into \<=2 concise sentences. - Replace
repetitive step logs with one summarizing phrase. - Truncate long token/credential strings to
"\<token\>" unless verbatim reuse is required. - Remove unused/expired credentials,
page_index/page_limit, verbose API dumps, and table borders. - Shrink GUARDRAILS to one bullet
unless multiple items are still critical. - Delete tool/API log output, greetings, meta prose, and
section headers that no longer contain content. - Keep only variables actively referenced in
upcoming steps; list each once in VARS. - Reference removal categories \[repetition\],
\[tool-logs\], \[meta\], \[formatting\] to prune similar lines. - Preserve factual continuity; never
invent or alter state variables. - Target summaries well under max_chars \| default(1500)
characters.

</div>

<div id="A4.SS0.SSS0.Px3.p63" class="ltx_para">

Critical Essentials: Always keep evidence-driven items required next session (e.g., tokens, ids,
emails, amounts, lists, paths, description strings, brief task status).

</div>

<div id="A4.SS0.SSS0.Px3.p64" class="ltx_para">

Output EXACTLY the following structure—nothing more:

</div>

<div id="A4.SS0.SSS0.Px3.p65" class="ltx_para">

\<HISTORY_SUMMARY\>

</div>

<div id="A4.SS0.SSS0.Px3.p66" class="ltx_para">

1\. REASONING One brief paragraph on key progress and rationale.

</div>

<div id="A4.SS0.SSS0.Px3.p67" class="ltx_para">

2\. VARS key=value pairs, comma-separated; only still-needed runtime values.

</div>

<div id="A4.SS0.SSS0.Px3.p68" class="ltx_para">

3\. TODO Bulleted next actions (\<=5).

</div>

<div id="A4.SS0.SSS0.Px3.p69" class="ltx_para">

4\. COMPLETED Bulleted finished subtasks (\<=5).

</div>

<div id="A4.SS0.SSS0.Px3.p70" class="ltx_para">

5\. GUARDRAILS Single concise bullet, or omit if none.

</div>

<div id="A4.SS0.SSS0.Px3.p71" class="ltx_para">

Return only the \<HISTORY_SUMMARY\> block—no additional commentary or input echoes.
<span id="A4.SS0.SSS0.Px3.p71.1.1"
class="ltx_ERROR undefined">{promptbox}</span>\[label=box:appworld_obs_compression\]AppWorld Prompt
for observation compression before optimization Your task is to generate a "Reasoning" and a
"Refined Observation" based on the inputs below.

</div>

<div id="A4.SS0.SSS0.Px3.p72" class="ltx_para">

In the "Reasoning", analyze the user instruction and history to identify what information from the
current observation is necessary to complete the remaining steps. Think about what parts can be
summarized or transformed to reduce length, while ensuring that future actions can still be executed
based on the refined observation alone.

</div>

<div id="A4.SS0.SSS0.Px3.p73" class="ltx_para">

In the "Refined Observation", include only the information that is minimal but sufficient for the
next steps.

</div>

<div id="A4.SS0.SSS0.Px3.p74" class="ltx_para">

\[Information source\] \# User Instruction task

</div>

<div id="A4.SS0.SSS0.Px3.p75" class="ltx_para">

\# History of interactions history

</div>

<div id="A4.SS0.SSS0.Px3.p76" class="ltx_para">

\# Observation at the current time step observation

</div>

<div id="A4.SS0.SSS0.Px3.p77" class="ltx_para">

\[Output format\] \# Reasoning … your reasoning for what matters and how to optimize it … \# Refined
Observation … reduced and actionable observation … <span id="A4.SS0.SSS0.Px3.p77.1.1"
class="ltx_ERROR undefined">{promptbox}</span>\[label=box:appworld_obs_optimized\]AppWorld Prompt
for observation compression after optimization (<span id="A4.SS0.SSS0.Px3.p77.1.2" class="ltx_text"
style="--ltx-fg-color:#D2691E;"> $`\overline{\underline{\textsc{ut}}}`$</span>) Your task: write two
sections—"Reasoning" and "Refined Observation".

</div>

<div id="A4.SS0.SSS0.Px3.p78" class="ltx_para">

1\. Reasoning - Examine task, history, and observation. - Decide exactly which parts of the
observation must be kept so the next agent step can succeed. - Note any need to paginate (page_limit
default = 5, page_index). - Justify any data you drop.

</div>

<div id="A4.SS0.SSS0.Px3.p79" class="ltx_para">

2\. Refined Observation - Contain only the minimal yet sufficient info for the next step. - Always
preserve: - Every endpoint that may be called, plus its full parameter list and defaults (especially
page_limit/page_index, auth tokens). - Response-schema fields referenced or likely needed later
(e.g., play_count, release_date, like_count, position, ids). - Raw data rows required for future
comparisons or loops; if summarising, keep at least all positive-match examples. - Never: - Omit
defaults that affect behaviour. - Declare parameters "not critical" without proof. - Hallucinate
endpoints or fields. - Replace machine-readable data with vague prose.

</div>

<div id="A4.SS0.SSS0.Px3.p80" class="ltx_para">

\[Information source\] \# User Instruction task

</div>

<div id="A4.SS0.SSS0.Px3.p81" class="ltx_para">

\# History of interactions history

</div>

<div id="A4.SS0.SSS0.Px3.p82" class="ltx_para">

\# Observation at the current time step observation

</div>

<div id="A4.SS0.SSS0.Px3.p83" class="ltx_para">

\[Output format\] \# Reasoning …concise analysis explaining what is kept/removed…

</div>

<div id="A4.SS0.SSS0.Px3.p84" class="ltx_para">

\# Refined Observation …trimmed yet complete observation ensuring future steps remain possible…

</div>

<div id="A4.SS0.SSS0.Px3.p85" class="ltx_para">

<span id="A4.SS0.SSS0.Px3.p85.1" class="ltx_ERROR undefined">{promptbox}</span>

\[label=box:appworld_obs_optimized_len\]AppWorld Prompt for observation compression after
optimization\
(<span id="A4.SS0.SSS0.Px3.p85.2.1" class="ltx_text" style="--ltx-fg-color:#D2691E;">
$`\overline{\underline{\textsc{ut}}}`$</span> <span id="A4.SS0.SSS0.Px3.p85.2.2" class="ltx_text"
style="--ltx-fg-color:#4B0082;"> $`\overline{\underline{\textsc{co}}}`$</span>) Your task: create
two sections—"Reasoning" and "Refined Observation".

</div>

<div id="A4.SS0.SSS0.Px3.p86" class="ltx_para">

1\. Reasoning (\<=40 words) - Briefly state what was kept and why; note dropped categories and any
pagination needs.

</div>

<div id="A4.SS0.SSS0.Px3.p87" class="ltx_para">

2\. Refined Observation (use ONLY the current observation) - Keep strictly necessary data for the
next step. - Always preserve, when present: - access_token or other auth values - page_limit,
page_index and other defaults that affect calls - every endpoint name; include parameters only if
required; description \<= 4 words - identifiers/fields needed for comparisons, loops, or API calls
(ids, titles, counts, paths, etc.) - Minimise length: - Delete unused fields and narrative text. -
Compress JSON/arrays (one object per line, no extra spaces). - Summarise long uniform lists with a
range/pattern when individual rows are not needed. - Never invent, alter, or omit a required
literal. - Exclude history, prior summaries, and meta comments.

</div>

<div id="A4.SS0.SSS0.Px3.p88" class="ltx_para">

\[Information source\] \# User Instruction task

</div>

<div id="A4.SS0.SSS0.Px3.p89" class="ltx_para">

\# History of interactions history

</div>

<div id="A4.SS0.SSS0.Px3.p90" class="ltx_para">

\# Observation at the current time step observation

</div>

<div id="A4.SS0.SSS0.Px3.p91" class="ltx_para">

\[Output format\] \# Reasoning …

</div>

<div id="A4.SS0.SSS0.Px3.p92" class="ltx_para">

\# Refined Observation …

</div>

<div class="ltx_pagination ltx_role_newpage">

</div>

<div id="A4.SS0.SSS0.Px3.p93" class="ltx_para ltx_noindent">

<div id="A4.SS0.SSS0.Px3.p96" class="ltx_para">

<span id="A4.SS0.SSS0.Px3.p96.1" class="ltx_ERROR undefined">{compressbox}</span>

\[label=compressbox:appworld_history\]AppWorld History Compression Example task: I got some
Splitwise group invitations over phone text messages yesterday. If their number is in my phone
contact book, accept it, otherwise delete those messages.

</div>

<div id="A4.SS0.SSS0.Px3.p97" class="ltx_para">

—————————————-

</div>

<div id="A4.SS0.SSS0.Px3.p98" class="ltx_para">

History Compression (Prompting baseline): \### REASONING

</div>

<div id="A4.SS0.SSS0.Px3.p99" class="ltx_para">

The agent began by identifying relevant apps for handling text messages and Splitwise invitations,
then explored the phone app’s APIs to manage messages and contacts. To search for Splitwise group
invitations received via text message, the agent determined it needed to log in to the phone app to
obtain an access token. Using credentials from the supervisor app, the agent successfully logged in
and retrieved the access token. The agent then calculated yesterday’s date range and searched for
text messages containing "Splitwise," retrieving a list of messages. The rationale for retaining
message details (sender name, phone number, contact_id, message content, sent_at) is to enable the
next step: checking if the sender’s number is in the contact book, so the agent can accept or delete
invitations as instructed. Earlier steps (API exploration, login, date calculation) directly enabled
the message search and data extraction.

</div>

<div id="A4.SS0.SSS0.Px3.p100" class="ltx_para">

\### COMPLETED

</div>

<div id="A4.SS0.SSS0.Px3.p101" class="ltx_para">

\- Identified relevant apps and APIs for the task. - Successfully logged in to the phone app and
obtained access token. - Calculated yesterday’s date range. - Searched and retrieved text messages
mentioning "Splitwise" from yesterday and beyond, including sender details and message content.

</div>

<div id="A4.SS0.SSS0.Px3.p102" class="ltx_para">

—————————————-

</div>

<div id="A4.SS0.SSS0.Px3.p103" class="ltx_para">

History Compression (ACON (utility step)): 1. REASONING - Identified "phone" app for managing text
messages and contacts. - Retrieved API docs to find endpoints for searching messages and contacts. -
Determined yesterday’s date (May 17, 2023) for filtering relevant messages. - Checked
"search_text_messages" API spec for required parameters. - Used supervisor app to obtain phone app
password. - Logged in to phone app to get access_token. - Searched for messages containing
"Splitwise" with page_limit=20, sorted by -created_at. - Located Splitwise group invitation
messages; sender contact_id used to determine if sender is in contact book.

</div>

<div id="A4.SS0.SSS0.Px3.p104" class="ltx_para">

2\. VARS \| name \| value \| purpose \| \|—————\|—————————\|——————\| \| access_token \|

\| Splitwise invitations from 2023-05-17 \| \| sender_info \| \[id:1857, name:"Alex White",
phone:"3707865891", id:null, name:"Anita Burch", phone:"3643463570"\] \| For contact book check \|

</div>

search_text_messages if more relevant messages may exist.

</div>

4\. COMPLETED - Identified relevant apps and APIs for task. - Retrieved phone app password from
supervisor. - Logged in to phone app and obtained access_token. - Searched and filtered text
messages for Splitwise invitations on target date. - Isolated relevant message IDs and sender info
for next steps.

</div>

<div id="A4.SS0.SSS0.Px3.p107" class="ltx_para">

5\. GUARDRAILS - Always re-declare VARS after memory reset. - Paginate message search until no more
results. - Validate API parameters before each call. - Avoid redundant logins and API doc
look-ups. - Only act on messages matching both query and target date.

</div>

class="ltx_ERROR undefined">{compressbox}</span>\[label=compressbox:qa_history\]8-objective QA
History Compression Example task: when was the last time the dodgers played yankees in the world
series?; where will be the next olympics be held?; who is tinker air force base named after?; who
does the voice of cruz on cars 3?; who’s the original singer of help me make it through the night?;
where does the movie mothers day take place?; what is the big gold dome in jerusalem?; when did
russia join the world economic forum?

</div>

<div id="A4.SS0.SSS0.Px3.p115" class="ltx_para">

—————————————-

</div>

<div id="A4.SS0.SSS0.Px3.p116" class="ltx_para">

History Compression (Prompting baseline): \### REASONING The agent began by addressing the user’s
multi-part query, starting with "when was the last time the Dodgers played Yankees in the World
Series?" Through Wikipedia searches, the agent identified 1981 as the most recent World Series
meeting between the Dodgers and Yankees. This answer was retained due to clear evidence in the
retrieved documents. The agent then attempted to answer "where will be the next Olympics be held?"
but initial searches returned irrelevant or outdated results (e.g., Amsterdam 1928, Buenos Aires
2018). Recognizing the lack of direct answers, the agent refined its search queries multiple times,
but still did not retrieve the current location for the next Olympics. The agent’s iterative
approach demonstrates a commitment to accuracy and highlights the need to adjust search strategies
when initial attempts fail. Retaining details of failed searches helps prevent repeated mistakes and
guides future queries.

</div>

<div id="A4.SS0.SSS0.Px3.p117" class="ltx_para">

\### COMPLETED - Identified the last time the Dodgers played the Yankees in the World Series: 1981.

</div>

Obs. Compression (Prompting baseline):

</div>

<div id="A4.SS0.SSS0.Px3.p152" class="ltx_para">

The Spotify API provides: - show_album_library: get user’s album library. - show_downloaded_songs:
get list of downloaded songs. - show_album: get details of a specific album.

</div>

any one of queue_position, song_id, album_id or playlist_id. If one of song_id, album_id or
